<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Identify and assess vendor dependencies

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>CloudCore example: remote shift in COVID

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Test non-IT response like IT systems

**Speaker Notes:**

While IT systems are often the core focus, effective disaster recovery must also consider non-technical assets. 

These include people, facilities, vendors, and business processes. Ignoring these aspects can create major gaps in your DR plan—even if your systems recover flawlessly.

Staff recovery plans include communication procedures, role reassignments, and remote work enablement. Facilities plans cover relocation to alternate sites, power needs, and access to critical equipment. 

Vendor dependencies should be reviewed to ensure they can continue delivering services or have backup providers.

CloudCore's DR plan includes remote work protocols for all employees, a contract with a coworking space for temporary office access, and agreements with multiple cloud vendors for redundant hosting. 

During the COVID-19 pandemic, they used these capabilities to transition to fully remote operations with minimal disruption.

These non-IT components should be documented clearly in the DR plan, tested regularly, and integrated with your IT recovery timelines.

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Keep updated with system changes

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Core part of DR documentation

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Useful for audits and stakeholder reviews

**Speaker Notes:**

To keep your strategy decisions organized and actionable, this course includes a downloadable Recovery Strategy Worksheet. 

This worksheet helps you align each business function or system with its recovery priority, method, and supporting resources. 

It brings together the outcomes of your BIA, risk assessment, and strategic planning in a single, easy-to-reference tool.

For each system or function, the worksheet prompts you to record its RTO/RPO, tier classification, selected recovery strategy, required resources (e.g., cloud instances, backup tools), and assigned owner. It also includes space for notes and change tracking.

CloudCore uses this worksheet as a live planning document. 

Their DR team refers to it during quarterly reviews and updates it whenever systems, vendors, or infrastructure change. It is also used during testing to validate that each recovery strategy performs as expected.

You should store this worksheet with your DR plan documentation and use it to brief stakeholders or auditors on how recovery priorities were determined.

## Lesson 10: Structuring Your DR Plan

### Slide 1

**Slide Content:**

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1600" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="0" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:srgbClr val="0033E4"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="Arial"/><a:sym typeface="Arial"/></a:rPr><a:t>Disaster Recovery Step by Step

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="4400" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:srgbClr val="2853E8"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Structure of the Disaster Recovery Plan

Section

Content Description

1. Introduction

Purpose, scope, plan owner, document control

2. Roles & Responsibilities

Recovery team structure, contacts, escalation path

3. Recovery Procedures

System-specific recovery instructions and sequences

4. Communication Plan

Stakeholder messages, timing, internal/external contacts

5. Alternate Site Procedures

Office relocation, remote work, equipment access

6. Vendor Coordination

Critical vendors, SLAs, emergency contacts

7. Appendices

BIA, risk register, strategy worksheet, test results

**Speaker Notes:**

Now that you’ve gathered all necessary information—critical functions, risks, priorities, and strategies—it’s time to create your Disaster Recovery Plan (DRP). This plan consolidates your findings into a formal, structured document that can be followed during a disruption. A well-organized DRP reduces confusion, supports coordinated recovery, and ensures consistent execution across departments.

The DRP should begin with an introduction outlining its purpose, scope, and approval status. 

Following that, include a section on roles and responsibilities—drawing from the team charter—and an overview of communication protocols. 

The core of the plan is the recovery procedures, which specify how to restore systems and processes according to their assigned recovery tier. 

This includes step-by-step instructions, contact lists, and system-specific recovery checklists.

Support sections may include alternate site procedures, vendor coordination steps, recovery tools, and forms. 

Appendices should contain the BIA summary, risk register, and completed recovery strategy worksheet. Store the plan in multiple secure locations and ensure all team members know how to access it. CloudCore organizes its DRP into a shared internal document with role-based access. Each team member has a personalized section tailored to their role, and the core plan is version-controlled with quarterly review notes.

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2400" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="white"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Assign clear ownership for execution

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2400" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0" err="1"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="white"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>CloudCore uses verified restore scripts

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2400" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="white"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Review and test each procedure regularly

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2400" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="white"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Update after system or vendor changes

**Speaker Notes:**

System recovery procedures are the heart of the DR plan. 

Each critical system or service identified in your BIA and strategy worksheet should have a dedicated set of recovery instructions. These procedures detail exactly what must be done, by whom, and in what order, to restore functionality.

Effective procedures begin with a brief system description, followed by prerequisites (e.g., access credentials, software images, backup location), and a step-by-step recovery process. Include verification steps to confirm successful recovery and post-recovery actions such as user validation or re-routing traffic.

Use clear, concise language that non-experts can follow during a stressful situation. 

Where possible, include screenshots, command examples, URLs, and diagrams. 

Assign ownership to specific team members or roles, not just “IT.”

CloudCore’s recovery procedures include one for the customer database, which covers verifying backup status in AWS, restoring snapshots, reinitializing the database service, and confirming API response via automated tests. These steps are documented in a dedicated system recovery guide, linked from the DRP.

Each procedure should be reviewed during DR testing to ensure accuracy and updated after every major system change.

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Define time-based escalation triggers

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Match contacts to DR responsibilities

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Delays occur because the right people cannot be reached in time

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Include primary/secondary contact methods

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="1" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0" err="1"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>CloudCore example: color-coded directory

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Update quarterly or after team changes

**Speaker Notes:**

A well-maintained contact directory is essential for executing the DR plan efficiently. 

During a disaster, delays often occur not because of technical issues, but because the right people cannot be reached in time. 

A centralized directory ensures the recovery team, executives, vendors, and key stakeholders can be contacted quickly.

The contact directory should include names, titles, roles, primary and secondary contact methods (e.g., mobile, email, messaging apps), and escalation order. You may also want to tag contacts by functional area or time zone.

Escalation paths describe when and how to involve higher-level management or external partners. For example, if a critical recovery fails after 30 minutes, the plan might require escalating to the CIO or engaging a cloud vendor's support escalation line. Define these paths clearly so the team doesn’t waste time deciding who to notify during a crisis.

CloudCore uses a color-coded contact sheet with role-based tabs and mobile numbers verified quarterly. Each recovery scenario in their plan includes a specific escalation condition and trigger time (e.g., “Escalate if SaaS platform unavailable after 15 minutes”).

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2400" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Download the editable DR Plan Template

**Speaker Notes:**

To streamline documentation, this course includes a fully editable Disaster Recovery Plan Template. The template aligns with the structure outlined earlier and includes pre-filled guidance to help you draft each section. 

It’s designed for clarity, flexibility, and audit-readiness. 

The template is divided into logical sections: general information, team structure, technical recovery procedures, communication protocols, non-IT recovery steps, and appendices. 

You can adapt it to your organization by removing or expanding sections based on complexity and scope.

Each section contains guidance text, placeholder examples (from CloudCore), and embedded links to relevant templates such as the BIA, risk register, and strategy worksheet. 

System recovery procedures are formatted to support checklist-style execution.

CloudCore uses this template as their live DR plan. 

It’s stored in their internal documentation system with permissions set based on role. They link directly to updated recovery scripts, vendor SLAs, and test logs, making the document a one-stop resource during disruption.

Download the template now and begin adapting it using your real data from Steps 1 to 5. It will evolve over time and serve as your central DR reference document.

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Prevents delays and builds trust

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Required by some regulations (e.g., GDPR)

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Avoids duplicated or missed recovery steps

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0" err="1"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>CloudCore uses pre-written updates and scripts

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Builds trust internally and externally

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Reduces panic and confusion

**Speaker Notes:**

During a disaster, effective communication can be the difference between calm coordination and widespread confusion. 

A well-planned communication strategy ensures that everyone—internal teams, leadership, vendors, and customers—receives accurate, timely information throughout the recovery process. Communication helps clarify what’s happening, who’s responsible, what actions are being taken, and what stakeholders should expect next. 

A DR communication plan prevents delays, reduces speculation, and builds trust. 

In some cases, legal or regulatory obligations may also require organizations to notify authorities or customers within specific timeframes following an incident. For example, many data protection regulations require breach notifications within 72 hours.

Poor communication during disruptions often leads to duplicated work, missed steps, or reputational damage. If employees don’t know where to report, customers don’t know what’s happening, and management is left guessing, recovery stalls and trust erodes. That’s why the communication plan is not a side task—it’s a critical piece of your disaster recovery architecture.

At CloudCore, when the SaaS platform goes down, the DR plan includes pre-written status updates for internal staff and customers. The Communications Lead activates these templates, coordinates with legal counsel if needed, and updates leadership every 30 minutes until services are restored. 

This prevents confusion and shows transparency.

**Slide Content:**

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1600" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="0" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:srgbClr val="0033E4"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="Arial"/><a:sym typeface="Arial"/></a:rPr><a:t>Disaster Recovery Step by Step

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="4400" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:srgbClr val="375FEA"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Identify Stakeholders and Notification Triggers

Trigger Event

Notification Time

App goes offline >15 mins

SMS/Email

Customer login issues

< 30 mins

Recovery complete

**Speaker Notes:**

The first step in developing a DR communication plan is identifying who needs to be informed, what they need to know, and when they need to know it. 

Stakeholders are typically grouped into four categories: internal teams (e.g., IT, operations), leadership, external vendors, partners, and customers or the public.

Once stakeholder groups are defined, outline the trigger events that initiate communication. These could include events like “critical system outage detected,” “failover begins,” or “data breach suspected.” 

For each event, define who will be notified, how, and within what timeframe. The key is speed, clarity, and consistency.

For example, when CloudCore’s primary region goes offline, internal IT is notified immediately, leadership is briefed within 10 minutes, and the communications team issues a customer update within 30 minutes. These triggers are mapped to communication actions, so no one has to decide ad hoc who to notify during an emergency.

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Customize templates by audience

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1600" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="0" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:srgbClr val="0033E4"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="Arial"/><a:sym typeface="Arial"/></a:rPr><a:t>Disaster Recovery Step by Step

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="3600" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:srgbClr val="2853E8"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Prepare Pre-Written Messages and Templates

**Speaker Notes:**

Pre-writing communication templates ensures that during a crisis, messages can be sent quickly and with confidence. 

These templates should cover the most common disaster scenarios and include language for both internal and external stakeholders.

Messages should be short, clear, and neutral. 

Avoid technical jargon and speculation. Focus on facts: what happened, what’s being done, and what people should expect next. 

You should prepare templates for incident acknowledgment, progress updates, resolution confirmation, and follow-up actions.

CloudCore maintains a library of message templates, each tied to specific incident types. For example, a system outage message to customers might read: “We are currently experiencing a service disruption affecting login functionality. Our technical team is working to resolve the issue and we’ll provide an update within 30 minutes.”

Customize templates by audience. Internal updates can be more technical and detailed, while customer-facing messages should emphasize service continuity, apologies if needed, and next steps.

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2000" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Match channels to audience needs

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2000" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0" err="1"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>CloudCore uses in-app + email alerts

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2000" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Keep internal and public info consistent

**Speaker Notes:**

Successful disaster communication depends on assigning clear roles and using reliable channels. 

Roles should include a Communications Lead, who coordinates the overall effort; 

Spokespersons, who are authorized to speak externally; 

and Support Coordinators, who assist with status updates, call routing, or FAQs.

Next, choose communication channels. Internally, this might include Slack, Teams, SMS, or phone trees. 

Externally, you may use email campaigns, website banners, customer support scripts, or social media posts. 

Always select channels based on your audience and their expectations.

At CloudCore, the Communications Lead is supported by the Customer Support Manager and a Legal Advisor. Updates are sent via email, in-app banners, and a status page. 

Internally, the DR team uses Slack and SMS for rapid updates. This setup allows for fast, role-specific communication without delays or missteps.

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="4400" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:srgbClr val="2853E8"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Use the Communication Plan Template

**Speaker Notes:**

To simplify the process of documenting your communication strategy, use the Communication Plan Template provided in this course. This editable template helps you organize stakeholder groups, trigger events, notification timelines, channels, and message owners.

Begin by listing your internal and external stakeholders. 

Then, for each trigger scenario, assign who is responsible for sending the message, how they will send it, and when. 

Include links to pre-approved message templates and escalation paths.

The template also includes a section to log communication tests. This ensures your plan is not only written, but practiced and kept up to date. 

At CloudCore, this template is reviewed quarterly during DR drills. Any issues with outdated contacts or unclear instructions are flagged and resolved.

This document becomes a vital reference during disruptions and gives leadership confidence that communication will not break down when it matters most.

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t> Builds staff confidence and coordination

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t> Supports audits, compliance, and certification

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t> CloudCore tests quarterly using real data

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t> Lessons from tests improve the plan

**Speaker Notes:**

A disaster recovery plan is only as good as its performance during a real incident. That’s why regular testing is a critical part of any DR program. 

Testing ensures that procedures work as intended, team members understand their responsibilities, tools and backups function properly, and gaps can be identified and addressed before an actual disruption occurs.

Without testing, your DR plan is simply theory. Even a well-written plan can fail due to expired credentials, outdated contact lists, misconfigured backups, or undocumented system changes. Regular testing uncovers these issues and gives the organization a chance to improve response time and coordination.

Testing also builds confidence across the organization. Team members who participate in drills are more likely to stay calm and act decisively during a real event. 

Leadership gains assurance that the organization is prepared, and audits or certification reviews are easier to pass when documented testing is in place.

At CloudCore, DR tests are conducted quarterly. These include both tabletop exercises and partial recovery drills. For example, they simulate a database corruption scenario, walk through the full restoration steps using the documented procedure, and review results against target RTO and RPO values. 

Post-test debriefs are held to refine the plan and update documentation.

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:fld id="{B463AAC3-1C03-4FDB-8D03-AB2469BAF3C4}" type="slidenum"><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1200" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" smtClean="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:pPr marL="0" marR="0" lvl="0" indent="0" algn="r" defTabSz="914400" rtl="0" eaLnBrk="1" fontAlgn="auto" latinLnBrk="0" hangingPunct="1"><a:lnSpc><a:spcPct val="100000"/></a:lnSpc><a:spcBef><a:spcPts val="0"/></a:spcBef><a:spcAft><a:spcPts val="0"/></a:spcAft><a:buClrTx/><a:buSzTx/><a:buFontTx/><a:buNone/><a:tabLst/><a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:t>1

### Slide 2

**Slide Content:**

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1600" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="0" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:srgbClr val="0033E4"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="Arial"/><a:sym typeface="Arial"/></a:rPr><a:t>Disaster Recovery Step by Step

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="6000" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:srgbClr val="2853E8"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t> Types of DR Tests

Test Type

Description

Complexity

Impact

Checklist

Verify readiness without action

Low

Minimal

Tabletop

Walk through scenario with no system change

Medium

Low

Simulation

Restore data/systems in test environment

High

Medium

Full Interruption

Shutdown real systems and execute recovery

Very High

High

**Speaker Notes:**

There are several types of disaster recovery tests, each offering a different level of realism and impact. Selecting the right type depends on your objectives, time availability, and organizational maturity. Testing should be scheduled regularly, with increasing complexity as your DR program matures.

Tabletop Exercises are structured discussions where the DR team walks through a simulated scenario step by step. No systems are touched, but roles, decisions, and communication paths are practiced.

Checklist Tests involve verifying whether all required DR documentation and assets are up to date—such as backups, credentials, or vendor contracts.

Simulation Tests go a step further by involving actual systems. For example, restoring a virtual server from backup to confirm that the process and data integrity are sound.

Full Interruption Tests are the most comprehensive and involve shutting down systems to simulate real outages. These require extensive planning and are often reserved for off-hours or non-production environments. CloudCore uses a mix of tabletop and simulation tests. Full interruption testing is done annually on staging environments to limit risk while validating complete recovery workflows.

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2000" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Set measurable benchmarks for success

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2000" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Restore SaaS platform within the 4-hour RTO

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2000" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Notify stakeholders within the expected time frame

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2000" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Track deviations and improvement areas

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2000" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0" err="1"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>CloudCore example: restore + notify in 2 hours

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2000" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Use post-test report for audit trail

**Speaker Notes:**

Before conducting a DR test, it’s important to define clear objectives. What exactly do you want to test and validate? 

Objectives can include verifying RTO/RPO targets, confirming system recovery steps, assessing communication procedures, or evaluating the DR team’s readiness.

Once objectives are defined, specify how success will be measured. 

For instance, was the SaaS platform restored within the 4-hour RTO? 

Did the Communications Lead notify stakeholders within the expected time frame? Were recovery scripts followed accurately? Having defined success criteria gives your team a benchmark to work toward and a framework for post-test evaluation. 

Even if the test reveals problems, it’s still a success if it leads to improved processes or documentation. Testing is about progress, not perfection.

At CloudCore, each test scenario includes a goal (e.g., restore customer database in under 2 hours), a checklist of expected actions, and a final report that measures performance against these goals. 

These reports are reviewed by leadership and stored as part of their audit trail.

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="0" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="Calibri" panose="020F0502020204030204" pitchFamily="34" charset="0"/></a:rPr><a:t>Meet post-test for team debrief

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="0" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="Calibri" panose="020F0502020204030204" pitchFamily="34" charset="0"/></a:rPr><a:t>Use real roles and documented steps

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="0" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="Calibri" panose="020F0502020204030204" pitchFamily="34" charset="0"/></a:rPr><a:t>Record delays, confusion, or errors

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="0" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="Calibri" panose="020F0502020204030204" pitchFamily="34" charset="0"/></a:rPr><a:t>CloudCore logs test scenarios + outcomes

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="0" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="Calibri" panose="020F0502020204030204" pitchFamily="34" charset="0"/></a:rPr><a:t>Update DR docs based on results

**Speaker Notes:**

When executing the test, ensure all participants understand their roles, the scope of the scenario, and the expected actions. 

Appoint a facilitator or test coordinator to guide the process, keep the timeline on track, and capture observations. For tabletop or checklist tests, the facilitator asks questions such as: “What is your first action when notified?” or “How would you confirm the database is restored?” 

During simulation tests, participants should follow documented procedures as if it were a real event, using test systems or backups where appropriate.

Record what went well and where confusion, delays, or errors occurred. Ask questions like: Did the team access the DR plan easily? Were contact details accurate? Did backup restoration steps match the plan? 

Keep detailed notes on these observations.

CloudCore logs all test results in a standardized template, noting the scenario tested, timeline, deviations, and recommended improvements. 

Team members then meet for a debrief to discuss lessons learned and update DR documents accordingly.

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Use as part of DR documentation

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Improves consistency across tests

**Speaker Notes:**

To ensure your test is planned and executed smoothly, use the DR Testing Checklist provided in this course. This downloadable template helps you track everything before, during, and after the test. 

It’s suitable for tabletop, simulation, or even full interruption testing.

The checklist includes steps for pre-test preparation (e.g., defining objectives, inviting participants), execution (e.g., following recovery procedures, capturing timelines), and post-test review (e.g., completing the test report, updating documentation).

At CloudCore, each DR drill begins with a completed checklist that is shared with all participants. 

After the test, they review each item, record lessons learned, and assign improvement tasks with due dates. This makes the process repeatable and improves accountability.

You should attach this checklist to your DR plan and test reports as evidence of continuous improvement. Use it as both a planning and learning tool.

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2200" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="white"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Avoid ad hoc decisions during activation

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2200" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="white"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Minimize confusion and downtime

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2200" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="white"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Document every step for review

**Speaker Notes:**

When a disaster strikes, the first step is activating your disaster recovery plan. 

Activation begins when an incident crosses predefined thresholds—such as extended downtime, a confirmed cyberattack, or a natural event that disables your infrastructure. 

The DR Coordinator or designated lead is responsible for declaring the incident and initiating the recovery protocol.

Activation should follow a consistent process: confirm the incident, assess its impact, notify stakeholders, and launch the recovery workflow according to the system recovery priorities outlined in your plan. Immediate coordination is critical—time wasted debating next steps can lead to unnecessary damage.

At CloudCore, DR activation is triggered when customer access to the SaaS platform is down for more than 10 minutes and the root cause is unresolved. 

The DR team receives an alert, the Communications Lead initiates internal updates, and system owners begin executing restoration steps. The DR plan should guide every action from this point—who does what, in what order, using what tools. 

Sticking to the documented procedures helps avoid chaos, ensure compliance, and support traceability for later analysis.

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Log every action and decision

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Begin restoring Tier 1 systems

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Set expectations with stakeholders early

**Speaker Notes:**

The first 24 hours of a disaster are often the most intense. Decisions made during this period set the tone for the entire recovery. 

The focus should be on ensuring safety, maintaining communication, and stabilizing operations.

Key actions include confirming team availability, assigning active recovery roles, coordinating communications, and starting system restorations based on priority. 

Log all decisions, changes, and issues encountered. Maintain a clear timeline of actions taken and people involved.

At CloudCore, the first hour involves team check-ins, incident briefing, and progress tracking via a shared dashboard. 

The goal is to restore Tier 1 services while keeping leadership and customers updated every 30 minutes. A command center—virtual or physical—is used to centralize updates and decision-making.

This early phase is also when customer trust is most at risk. Even if full recovery isn’t yet possible, showing control and responsiveness can preserve confidence. 

Pre-approved messaging and leadership visibility play a crucial role here.

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2600" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Update DR plan and templates afterward

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2600" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Show audit trail of lessons learned

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2600" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Support continuous improvement mindset

**Speaker Notes:**

Once the recovery is complete and systems are stable, conduct a post-incident review—also known as a lessons learned session. 

The purpose is to understand what happened, how the response was managed, what worked, what failed, and what should be improved in the future.

This review should include all involved stakeholders, including recovery team members, business leaders, and support staff. 

Start with a timeline of the incident: when it started, when it was declared, and when services were restored. Then walk through each response phase, documenting successes, delays, confusion, and unexpected challenges.

At CloudCore, a structured post-incident meeting is held within 5 business days of the event. 

The team uses a standard report template to record what was observed and to assign improvement tasks. Issues like missing tools, outdated procedures, or unclear roles are logged and prioritized for correction.

The review also provides an opportunity to update the DR plan, training content, and contact lists. 

It shows a culture of continuous improvement and readiness, not just compliance.

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2400" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="white"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Store securely with DR documentation

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2400" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="white"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Supports audits, leadership reporting, and training

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2400" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="white"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Use real data to drive DR updates

**Speaker Notes:**

The Post-Incident Report is a formal document that summarizes the entire disaster event and recovery process. It serves as a permanent record for internal learning, audits, and regulatory reviews. 

This report captures the timeline of the event, recovery steps taken, communication actions, deviations from the DR plan, and recommendations for improvement. 

The report should include a summary of the incident (type, impact, systems affected), a breakdown of each recovery phase, roles involved, communication timelines, and recovery outcomes (whether RTOs and RPOs were met). Highlight lessons learned and any systemic issues discovered.

CloudCore’s post-incident reports are structured and standardized. Each report includes sections for executive summary, detailed timeline, technical notes, team feedback, and a list of proposed actions with deadlines and assigned owners. 

Reports are stored securely and presented to the executive team during quarterly risk reviews.

Creating this document not only improves accountability, but also ensures that changes to the DR plan are based on real incidents—not assumptions. It supports training, audits, and helps justify future investments in resilience.

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Include CloudCore sample as a reference

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Use after real events or major drills

**Speaker Notes:**

To make your post-incident documentation easier and more consistent, use the Post-Incident Report Template provided in this course. This template walks you through every section needed to conduct an effective review and produce a professional, audit-ready report. 

The template includes fields for incident details, impacted systems, recovery timeline, communication actions, stakeholder feedback, and an improvement tracker. 

It also includes sections for executive summary and root cause analysis if applicable.

Start by completing the timeline based on system logs, team notes, and communications. 

Then review each recovery action against the DR plan—was it followed, skipped, or modified? If objectives were missed, document why and what needs to change.

CloudCore’s completed template includes one example: a simulated SaaS platform outage. 

It details how response time lagged due to outdated credentials, which led to a corrective action item to rotate passwords quarterly and audit account access.

Use this template after real incidents or simulation tests. It will help you maintain a culture of learning, transparency, and readiness.

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2400" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Reference CloudCore’s completed DRP

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2400" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Use this as benchmark to your own work

**Speaker Notes:**

This final project gives you the opportunity to apply everything you've learned throughout the course by building a complete, structured, and actionable Disaster Recovery Plan (DRP). 

This project is more than an academic exercise—it’s designed to give you a real-world deliverable that can be used within your organization or shared during job interviews, audits, or certifications.

You can choose to build a DR plan for your current organization, a past employer, or use the CloudCore Technologies case study we've followed throughout the course. 

Regardless of your choice, the key is to work through each step using the templates provided to document your work in a practical, professional format.The final plan should include all major components: objectives, team roles, BIA outputs, risk assessment results, recovery strategies, system procedures, communication plans, testing protocols, and continuous improvement sections. 

Each section builds on previous assignments, so if you’ve completed them progressively, you’ve already done most of the work.

CloudCore’s example DR plan will be available as a reference. It includes completed templates and filled-out worksheets showing how each part aligns with recovery goals and team responsibilities. 

You can use this as inspiration or to benchmark your own work.

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2400" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Appendices with all filled templates

**Speaker Notes:**

Your final DR plan should be organized and structured in a way that enables it to be used quickly during an emergency, easily reviewed by leadership, and updated as systems evolve. It should include both strategic and technical elements and be grounded in the templates you’ve completed during the course.

Start with a cover page and version control table. Include a short executive summary, scope, and objectives. 

Next, add your team structure and roles, ideally using the DR Team Charter. 

Your Business Impact Analysis (BIA) and Risk Register should follow, highlighting priorities and recovery targets.

Then, move into the core recovery strategies and system-specific procedures. This section may be the longest but is also the most actionable during a real incident. Include recovery steps, tools needed, and ownership.

Finally, add the communication plan, testing schedule, and continuous improvement activities. 

Attach all templates as appendices or link to them if hosted online. Keep formatting simple and legible—use headers, checklists, and tables where appropriate.

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="4400" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:srgbClr val="2853E8"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Assignment Instructions and Submission

**Speaker Notes:**

To complete the project, use the Final Project Checklist provided in the resources section. 

This checklist summarizes everything your DR plan should contain and allows you to verify completion of each part. You can work directly in the Disaster Recovery Plan Template or use your own formatting if preferred.

If you're using CloudCore Technologies as your case, reference the sample data provided and modify it with your own additions or analysis. 

If you’re using your actual organization, be sure not to include confidential data—use anonymized names or general descriptions where needed.

Once complete, export your DR plan as a PDF or Word document. 

If submitting as part of the course (e.g., in a cohort or instructor-led version), upload it to the final assignment portal. 

Otherwise, keep it for your own use, and consider presenting it internally or reviewing it with your DR team.

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Assign review tasks by section or role

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Updates are logged and versioned

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Present to leadership as part of regular risk 

**Speaker Notes:**

A disaster recovery plan is not a one-time task—it must evolve with your organization. Systems change, staff turn over, vendors are replaced, and risks shift over time. An outdated plan can create a false sense of security and lead to failure when it matters most. Sustaining the DR plan requires a structured approach to reviewing, updating, and validating all components regularly.

The first step is to define a review schedule. At a minimum, the DR plan should be reviewed annually, but more frequent reviews are recommended if your environment changes rapidly. Any significant change—like a new system deployment, a shift to a new cloud provider, or a change in organizational structure—should trigger an immediate plan update.

Establish a process for version control so you can track changes over time and revert to older versions if needed. 

Every plan update should be approved by the DR lead and shared with the recovery team.

CloudCore has implemented a quarterly review cycle. 

Each team lead is responsible for checking their assigned section of the DR plan and confirming that contacts, procedures, and systems are still accurate. 

These updates are logged, versioned, and presented to leadership as part of regular risk and continuity reporting.

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Use incident simulations as learning events

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Test backups and procedures frequently

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Practice builds calm and competence

**Speaker Notes:**

Readiness is not achieved through documentation alone—it requires continuous awareness, training, and practice. 

To maintain DR effectiveness, integrate recovery responsibilities into regular operations, train new team members, and rehearse the plan through frequent testing and simulated incidents. Employees involved in the DR process should understand their responsibilities, the tools they need to use, and the procedures to follow. 

When onboarding new staff into recovery roles, provide immediate training using the DR plan, past incident reports, and test results. 

Awareness should extend beyond the DR team—staff across departments should know how to respond when incidents occur and how to escalate appropriately.

At CloudCore, DR readiness is maintained through quarterly tabletop exercises and annual simulation drills. New engineers are introduced to the DR plan within their first 30 days. 

IT managers are tasked with testing backup restores monthly, and communications staff rehearse stakeholder messaging during incident simulations.

Repetition builds muscle memory. 

Regular practice helps individuals internalize their role and allows teams to operate calmly and efficiently under pressure.

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1600" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="0" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:srgbClr val="0033E4"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="Arial"/><a:sym typeface="Arial"/></a:rPr><a:t>Disaster Recovery Step by Step

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="4400" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:srgbClr val="2853E8"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Monitor Metrics and Track Improvements

**Speaker Notes:**

Sustaining and improving your DR plan also means measuring performance and using data to guide future updates. Recovery metrics help answer: How fast did we respond? Did we meet our RTO and RPO targets? How effective was our communication? What issues repeated from past tests? 

Track metrics like recovery time actuals (versus RTO), success rate of recovery procedures, communication delivery time, test participation rates, and number of open vs. closed action items from post-incident reviews. 

Use this data to create internal dashboards or simple reports that are reviewed regularly by leadership. 

Highlight areas of improvement, risks that remain unaddressed, or training needs that emerge over time. 

Showing progress not only supports audits—it helps justify investments in tools, staff, or automation.

CloudCore tracks four key DR KPIs: time to restore Tier 1 systems, percent of successful backup restores, internal comms delivery time, and open action items from tests. Each quarter, they review these metrics as part of their business continuity dashboard.

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t> Encourage openness about risks and gaps

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t> Combine structure with adaptability 

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t> Build teams that expect and overcome disruption

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t> CloudCore treats DR as a company habit

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="2800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="black"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t> Discuss recovery KPIs like any business metric

**Speaker Notes:**

True disaster recovery readiness goes beyond plans and procedures—it’s a mindset. 

Building a culture of resilience means embedding DR awareness into the everyday behavior, values, and decision-making of your organization. Everyone, from senior leadership to new hires, should understand that disruption is a possibility and preparedness is a responsibility.

This begins with leadership setting the tone—prioritizing resilience, funding DR activities, and participating in drills. 

Managers should encourage teams to report vulnerabilities, test backup procedures, and discuss lessons learned from past incidents. 

Employees should feel empowered to ask questions, suggest improvements, and escalate issues quickly. 

Resilience also means balancing preparedness with adaptability. Not every event will match your playbook exactly. 

A resilient team can improvise within a structured response framework, making smart decisions based on shared principles and a clear understanding of what’s at stake.

At CloudCore, resilience is treated as part of company culture. 

DR training is incorporated into all-hands meetings, leaders discuss readiness metrics alongside performance KPIs, and continuous improvement is built into workflows. The message is clear: disruptions are inevitable—what matters is how you respond.

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="white"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Build resilience into company culture

<a:defRPr/></a:pPr><a:r><a:rPr kumimoji="0" lang="en-US" sz="1800" b="0" i="0" u="none" strike="noStrike" kern="1200" cap="none" spc="0" normalizeH="0" baseline="0" noProof="0" dirty="0"><a:ln><a:noFill/></a:ln><a:solidFill><a:prstClr val="white"/></a:solidFill><a:effectLst/><a:uLnTx/><a:uFillTx/><a:latin typeface="Aptos" panose="02110004020202020204"/><a:ea typeface="+mn-ea"/><a:cs typeface="+mn-cs"/></a:rPr><a:t>Be ready—not just compliant—when disruption comes

**Speaker Notes:**

Congratulations on completing Disaster Recovery Step by Step with Templates. You’ve worked through a structured, comprehensive framework for designing, implementing, and sustaining a disaster recovery (DR) plan that’s not only compliant and actionable—but built to work in the real world. 

Whether you’re building your first DR plan or refining an existing one, you now have the tools, templates, and confidence to lead your organization through uncertainty and back to stability.Throughout the course, you’ve learned to identify critical business functions and their dependencies through a Business Impact Analysis. You’ve assessed threats and vulnerabilities using structured risk assessment methods. You’ve defined and prioritized recovery strategies tailored to system criticality, and you’ve documented these decisions using the Recovery Strategy Worksheet. You’ve also built a formal DR Plan—covering technical procedures, communication protocols, and team responsibilities. You’ve explored how to conduct various types of DR tests, measure recovery effectiveness, and capture lessons learned through post-incident reviews and reports. 

Most importantly, you’ve built the habit of continuous improvement, ensuring that your plan evolves as your business and technology landscape changes.

The templates you’ve completed—including the DR Plan, BIA, Risk Register, Communication Plan, and Post-Incident Report—can now serve as a foundation for future updates, audits, and team onboarding. 

If you’ve completed the final project, you now have a tangible DR plan to present, share, or use within your organization. CloudCore Technologies served as your guide along the way, illustrating how a mid-sized SaaS company puts theory into practice. 

Now it’s your turn to apply these lessons in your own environment and continue building a culture of resilience.

Disasters may be unpredictable—but with preparation, your response doesn’t have to be.
